# Samsung Galaxy Tab S7+ Wi-Fi \(SM-T970, gts7xlwifi\), single-core platform bootstrap

## Configuration {#configuration}

`host-verified`

Components: `ArmDTPlatform`.

### Hardware and dependencies

- ADB reports SM-T970, gts7xlwifi, SM8250, board platform kona and hardware qcom.
- The target OS is iPadOS 17.7.11, build 21H461, matching the local IPSW BuildManifest.plist.
- SoC feed: FFT-infra/UniXNU-sm8250-common at 166235ec2480ff0992627b7aafa15c65ce6b4efa; reviewed with UniXNU 04b4e558bcded5213c2d802984862dc062f28257.

### Configuration

- board.toml uses schema\_version=2, name/kit=sm-t970, soc=SM8250, soc\_source=sm8250 and os\_profile=ipados-17.7.11-21H461.
- Complete component selection: ArmDTPlatform, ArmCpu, SoftwareAES, XnuPrelink and DarwinBoot. The inherited archive list is preserved.
- Config fragments are ARM64 common, SM8250 and iPadOS in that order, followed by sm-t970\_defconfig. Patch series are arch, soc, os, device; arrays replace inherited arrays.
- feeds.toml explicitly pins the five selected components in addition to the SoC source. The selection snapshot fixes the reviewed UniXNU revision and ARM64/OS profile hashes.

### Procedure

- From the sibling UniXNU checkout run python3.14 ./unixnu defconfig sm-t970\_defconfig -D ../UniXNU-sm-t970, fetch the six selected SoC/component sources, then run status --json and tree.
- Run python3.14 ../UniXNU-sm-t970/scripts/check-platform.py --unixnu . to compare DT/ADT bindings with the captured hardware.

### Development findings

- BOOT5385b6ce owner photo03:00:42 shows three pending queues drained, MG done134, BB161, SB75-&gt;78, display open6/fail0/swap0. No constrained head means Q cap zero is absent data, not a measured cached limit. v22 reuses exact v21 kernel with existing stack mode1 to locate current BB/SB main waits; host image checks pass, hardware stack result pending.
- v21 BCM2837 EDGE width fallback uses registered SMP CPU/cluster counts only when E/P counts are both zero. Previous BOOT705d4160 has all eight ADT CPU cluster-type absent \(SMP\), cached constrained cap0 and callback progress. Actual prepared-function ASan/UBSan tests cover SMP1/8, E/P precedence and non-BCM guard; kernel/prelink/FAT checks pass. BOOT5385b6ce hardware result pending; this is not validated heterogeneous scheduling or desktop. See evidence/display/edge-smp-v21.
- BOOT cfbd84ba photo confirms stage4: ID/VALID/S2CR accepted, direct FAULT restore200ff succeeded without fallback, allRestored/mappingsUnchanged=1 and GFSR=0. Six context states captured; CB0..4 already configured with FSR400 of unknown age, CB73 SCTLR3ab3a706 is not a verified free bank. No context writes, DMA or UFS commands occurred.
- Combined field diagnostic BOOT cfbd84ba34fc03b50661b46c9e22fe59c43ba651bca6967da49f1df1ec373e75 prepared from source7ee2918/kernel53e9e68c. Thirteen host tests, input-stable kernel build, independent FAT/arguments/FD checks and DT/ADT check passed. - Prepared a combined single-slot field qualification after ee45890a: test VALID only under unchanged FAULT policy; test S2CR only with SMR invalid; restore with a bounded Qualcomm-aware fallback if necessary; compare all protected mappings; only then read six context states. No UFS commands, DMA route grants, queue/context/global/clock/SCM writes. Field results and restore flags still require exact-image device observation.

- Prepared a single-slot inactive SMR ID-field test, based on the complete 933a5d77 handoff observation. Rechecks mappings, writes only invalid ID a5 at offset958, restores zero and compares protected state; both writes fenced. No VALID/S2CR/CB/GCC/HCI/SCM/DMA changes. Outcome is not yet observed.

The sole valid SMR 84020820/S2CR 4 exactly matches the captured display SID 0820 mask 0402; preserve slot 04 and CB4. Candidate residues in invalid slots 02/03 are not active routes. No DMA, SCSI or root read was attempted.

- board.toml now points the OS layer of patch\_series at device:patches/xnu/os-series so the two required XnuPrelink ABI patches still apply while zzz-boot-progress.patch, which requires sheng's XNU\_SHENG\_PMGR hook, is omitted. The applied set is 60 patches.
- The measured boot partition is 71303168 bytes, so board.toml \[payload\] size\_mib dropped from 192 to 56; the 192 MiB payload could never be flashed. 56 MiB holds the 51.9 MB prelinked kernel plus ADT and trust cache with about 8 MB margin.
- board.toml \[payload\] now passes t970-novc and enables panic\_screen for the bring-up images; both are diagnostic defaults that must be revisited once a real console exists.
- The diagnostic tree at commit 66abd1f carries the t970-novc boot argument, panic\_screen and the three console-bypass patches; the checkpoint evidence revisions name that commit in full.

- Enabled CONFIG\_XNU\_SHENG\_PMGR=y in sm-t970\_defconfig, added the PMGR archive and sheng-pmgr=2 boot argument, and regenerated .config before the kernel build.
- feeds.toml now pins the SM8250 UFS fork and unixnu.lock records it, but no component selects it; board.toml and sm-t970\_defconfig remain in their buildable pre-UFS state.
- board.toml now carries the measured T970 \[userdata\] geometry and the root-volume UUID for the ADT; the UFS component selection, storage archives and native-ufs boot arguments used for the size measurement were reverted so the default build stays inside the BOOT partition.
- board.toml selects ShengUfs and VirtioMmioBlock, adds the empty-policy VerifiedRoot archive entry, and boots with sheng-pmgr=2, native-ufs-root=1 and no stream override \(default 0x60\); sm-t970\_defconfig enables CONFIG\_XNU\_NATIVE\_UFS and CONFIG\_XNU\_VERIFIED\_ROOT\_PROBE. The temporary MassStorage boot-option firmware patch was reverted.
- board.toml now passes t970-smmu-dump=1 together with native-ufs-root=1 for the SMMU table diagnostic; the flag only triggers the arm\_init dump and must be removed once the UFS stream is resolved.
- board.toml keeps native-ufs-root=1 with the default stream \(no override\) and selects component:ShengUfs only; the temporary MassStorage, smmu-dump and local-archive experiments are not part of the default boot path.
- The build currently selects the board-local ShengUfs archive \(component removed, zz-build-shengufs-local.py\), enables CONFIG\_XNU\_NATIVE\_UFS/VERIFIED\_ROOT\_PROBE and boots with native-ufs-root=1 plus t970-ufs-auto-stream=1. The kernel links and produces a 62.7 MB boot image.
- board.toml passes t970-ufs-no-smmu=1 for the last experiment; it is a diagnostic switch alongside native-ufs-root=1 and does not enable any storage publication.
- board.toml adds &\#123;uefi&\#125;/Silicon/Samsung to \[firmware\] packages so SamsungPkg resolves; the boot image continues to use the RELEASE firmware target.
- board.toml currently boots with t970-ufs-image=1 \(the read-only survey image\) and panic\_screen; the survey BOOT is c404ce26 and the image source revision is 09bff63.

### Completion criteria

- A board.toml with matching device identity resolves the selected SoC and OS profiles and pinned sources.

### Current limits

- Repository completion and removal of the example marker permit host builds; they do not certify a UEFI launch or an iPadOS boot.
- Apple kernelcache/private ABI, trust-cache, Data seed and XNU image compilation remain separate later-stage work.

### Next action

- Continue with audited SM8250 storage inputs and isolated images; retain exact source/image hashes and hardware observations.

## Firmware and boot {#firmware}

`host-verified`

Components: `DarwinBoot`.

### Hardware and dependencies

- Stock bootloader property: T970ZCU2CVK1.
- Stock build fingerprint: samsung/gts7xlwifizc/gts7xlwifi:12/SP2A.220305.013/T970ZCU2CVK1:user/release-keys.
- Boot properties report ro.boot.flash.locked=0, ro.boot.verifiedbootstate=orange and ro.boot.warranty\_bit=1.
- Live DT /psci declares arm,psci-1.0 with method smc; CPUs declare enable-method psci.
- Read-only inspection of boot and recovery headers reports ANDROID\! magic, header version 2 and page size 4096 bytes.

### Configuration

- board firmware.config=gts7xlwifi, dsc=gts7xlwifiPkg/gts7xlwifi.dsc and fd=Build/gts7xlwifiPkg/&\#123;target&\#125;\_CLANGPDB/FV/SILICIUM\_UEFI.fd point to the prepared pinned Mu-Silicium tree.
- prepare-uefi.py validates each pinned submodule and stock input hash, then stages the committed overlay; build-uefi-candidate.py exercises the official firmware builder without booting.

### Procedure

- The existing Android shell transcript records getprop ro.bootloader and getprop ro.build.fingerprint.
- Collect selected boot properties, the live DT and the first 4096 bytes of boot/recovery with the inventory script; only parsed header fields are saved.

### Development findings

- BOOT 933a5d77 device screen is confirmed through END slots=87. Corrected SM8250 GCC gate passes; HCE=1 HCS=10f IS=400, both queue run-stop bits set and both doorbells zero, UTRLBA=9b296000. The sole valid SMR 84020820/S2CR 4 exactly matches the captured display SID 0820 mask 0402; preserve slot 04 and CB4. Candidate residues in invalid slots 02/03 are not active routes. No DMA, SCSI or root read was attempted.
- Prepared and independently verified BOOT 933a5d776edb2500be5c268b93e64f2cfec53fee6e12cebbb39869a8d7050deb from source 58bdac8 and kernel 147f8d3b; DT/ADT checks passed. Patched Heimdall uploaded BOOT only with exit 0. The new handoff screen and full-table END marker await device observation; no UFS DMA/root success claimed.
- Prepared t970-ufs-handoff=1: corrected board-local SM8250 clock diagnostics from Samsung gcc-kona.c, removed SM8550-only memory-PHY GDSC reads, capture all bounded raw SMR/S2CR slots and guarded read-only HCI/queue state. No clock, PHY, SCM, SMMU, DMA or queue writes; use the exact tested baseline FD. Host and device results remain separate.
- User confirmed and photographed the expected T970 SMMUv2 RO stop for BOOT 7f729c2a: sCR0=002d0406 ID0=4c017e57 ID1=6000004a ID2=00005111 GFSR=0; 87 groups scanned, 74 context banks, no candidate match for 0060/00e0/04e0. MMIO observation is confirmed; SMR writability, actual UFS routing, DMA and root reads are not.
- Prepared BOOT 7f729c2a6ee8ee11b6eb5d4ff7ec23171b261854151578e36737d866a62391e2 from source 23d54b9 and kernel cca9bbb8. Independent FAT/kernel/arguments and packed FD checks passed, as did nine host tests and CPU0 DT/ADT checks. Patched Heimdall uploaded BOOT only with exit 0; device screen/register observation is pending. This is not a UFS/DMA/root-volume success.
- Resumed at 47b113f. New default diagnostic t970-smmu-report=1 reads the measured SMMUv2 window before any controller/queue/DMA/root setup and stops on a labeled panic. It retires the unflashed all-slot write scan, separates sCR0 from ID0/1/2, honors EXIDS, and does not treat ignored writes or prior SCM markers as proof that secure-world routes are exhausted.
- The prepared SEC patch rebuild passed; built FV inspection confirms TzDxe/PILDxe/PILProxyDxe absent and AdvancedLogDxe present.
- The handed-off runtime workaround excludes TzDxe, PILDxe and PILProxyDxe from DSC/FDF dispatch: the handoff reports TEE and missing-UFS partition assertions before the menu. The SEC x0 HOB patch is now hash-checked and automatically prepared. These reports do not certify TEE/PIL functionality.
- Handoff follow-up: prepare-uefi.py now applies or recognizes the SEC x0-to-HOB patch using its exact patch hash and four pre/postimage hashes; clean upstream application and a second idempotent run passed. The cached Mu source modifications are therefore reproducible rather than implicit workspace state.
- The normal unixnu build firmware command passed on committed sources; the 3 MiB FD contains all four UniXNU/DarwinBoot modules plus DiskIo, FAT and Unicode collation \(7 checked modules among 91 FV files\).
- A T970-specific overlay selects the pinned Kona support, original numeric configuration and a nonoverlapping 44-region map derived from the stock 45-region map; Android SEC LOG scratch is omitted because it overlaps FV/FD/stack and Mu would halt on it.
- 44 vendor modules use 84 exact PE32/DEPEX/RAW inputs from this T970 XBL. Vendor UFS/USB DMA dispatch is omitted from the CPU0/initrd candidate.
- DSC and FDF include UniXNU, UniXNUDxe, DarwinBoot and UniXNURamDiskDxe; DiskIo, FAT and Unicode collation are present. The actual EDK build produced a 3145728-byte FD before this implementation commit; a committed-source build report is the next check.
- Read-only XBL extraction validates FV/FFS header checksums and decompresses its gzip-guided volume. The snapshot has 121 FFS records, 94 PE32 assets and 45 platform regions. Stock FD is 0x9fc00000+0x300000; stack is 0x9ff90000+0x40000. These constants do not verify a new UEFI launch.
- The follow-up read-only inventory hashes 43 stock assets \(6 GPU, 8 Wi-Fi, 2 Bluetooth, 27 audio\). This expands the original two-file inventory; none is selected by the CPU0 bootstrap.
- The archived properties identify an Android 12 stock build and bootloader T970ZCU2CVK1.
- The running Android environment has Magisk root and reports an unlocked/orange boot state; this does not verify a UEFI launch method.
- The template firmware integration slots are retained as a table with only known package roots; board.example=true prevents treating the staging configuration as boot-ready.
- Reviewed Project-Silicium/Mu-Silicium commit 4bd8ae83bf37de41777f19e0777db70f59383a6d: KonaPkg and Samsung r8qPkg exist, but no T970/gts7xlwifi platform/config was found in the inspected upstream directories.
- The r8q reference declares Android header v1 and 1080x2400 display geometry, while the T970 inventory reports header v2 and 2800x1752 modes. No reference-board boot parameters or firmware path were adopted.
- Step 3 audit confirms firmware.config, firmware.dsc and firmware.fd are absent. Only package search roots are configured; the known GPU manifest entries are inventory, not a complete enabled-firmware integration.
- The firmware log exporter is now wired: AdvancedLogDxe \(FFS 8D4E7F50-3A2C-4C9A-9E1B-50C4C8472D41\) is in the DSC Components and FDF, AdvancedLoggerAccessLib and QrEncoderLib come from Common/Mu, PcdUniXNUDeviceModel is gts7xlwifi, and board \[payload\] tools adds the Tools entry.
- AdvancedLoggerLib is mapped for SEC \(PeilessArm\), DXE\_CORE, DXE\_DRIVER, DXE\_RUNTIME\_DRIVER, UEFI\_DRIVER and UEFI\_APPLICATION, with AdvancedLoggerHdwPortLib over SerialPortLib and BaseDebugLibAdvancedLogger only in DEBUG. PcdAdvancedLoggerBase stays 0, so the DXE Core library reserves the 4 MiB buffer instead of a fabricated fixed address; SEC and DEBUG screen output still reach FrameBufferSerialPortLib.
- A DEBUG rebuild with the exporter produced a 3145728-byte FD containing 92 FV files, including the exporter GUID, and FVMAIN.inf lists it. The earlier 91-file, 6108b542... FD is superseded.
- Committed-source rebuild with the exporter produced a 3145728-byte FD \(sha256 94709bbe5b7ecc1a78cb6041889258d9c537b096ae04bce41252ad57f4d8d30a\) whose FV holds 92 files and the exporter FFS.
- Step 5 records the two tree inputs as distinct artifacts. The bootloader FDT captured read-only from /sys/firmware/fdt is 837154 bytes \(86c14aa3...\) with 4533 nodes, 43 reserved-memory entries and a /chosen carrying bootargs, kaslr-seed and initrd range; two captures are byte-identical. The blob stays in ignored storage because /chosen holds identity and boot state; only hashes, structure, memory ranges and property names are archived.
- scripts/check-hardware-description.py rebuilds the board DTS and ADT with artificial identity 1, checks dram-base/size, CPU0 affinity and 19.2 MHz timebase, GIC registers and stride, rejects a firmware DTB equal to the board build or a board DTS carrying /chosen, and checks the firmware DSC/FDF plus the pinned base configuration for DiskIo, FAT and Unicode collation.
- The payload command was probed at the Step 5 commit: it reaches the payload step and stops only at the missing kernel/kernel.macho, so the board DTS/ADT and OS-profile side is not the blocker; the XNU kernel build is the next required input.
- The board package now carries Sm8250UfsPhyTables.h, the extracted SM8250 QMP UFS PHY register sequences, but the platform DSC still does not build any UFS DXE; no firmware image has been rebuilt or booted with it.
- The T970 firmware now builds Samsung UfsDxe plus a board PlatformUfsLib: the SM8250 QMP UFS PHY sequences are replayed after mapping GCC, PHY, controller and ICE windows into the UEFI page tables. On hardware the stage markers reach the final green pair \(PHY replay and PCS ready\), then the device falls back to the Samsung bootloader warning screen before XNU starts.
- GCC block writes hang the non-secure bus, so the board library skips them and relies on ABL's existing UFS power; the workspace marker sequence \(magenta/orange/cyan/teal/yellow/orange/purple/blue/deep blue/green/green\) is the reproducible evidence path for this bring-up.
- The board PlatformUfsLib now also issues QCOM\_SCM\_SVC\_MP / MP\_RESTORE\_SEC\_CFG over SMC \(0x02000000 \| \(svc&lt;&lt;8\) \| cmd, arginfo 2\) after the PHY succeeds. On hardware the first call \(device\_id 0\) returns non-zero \(red marker\) and the second does not complete, so the secure monitor does not restore the UFS SMMU configuration through this interface.

### Completion criteria

- A board-specific UEFI image starts reproducibly and produces an accessible boot log.

### Current limits

- UEFI launch, actual GOP/stride/pixel format, initrd discovery, runtime memory allocations and boot-services exit are untested. The running boot image is Magisk-modified.
- A host-built T970-specific candidate is now selected; it is not an upstream-certified or device-verified T970 port.

### Next action

- Audit T970 handoff against the public T733 vendor integration and umi inherited-state path using T970-owned inputs; old experimental SCM/PHY conclusions are not qualified.

## CPU and interrupts {#cpu}

`implemented`

Components: `ArmCpu`.

### Hardware and dependencies

- Android exposes CPUs 0-7. Live DT affinity values are 0x000, 0x100, 0x200, 0x300, 0x400, 0x500, 0x600 and 0x700.
- CPU frequency policies group CPUs 0-3, 4-6 and 7, with reported maxima 1804800, 2419200 and 3091200 kHz respectively.
- GICv3 distributor is 0x17a00000 + 0x10000; redistributor region is 0x17a60000 + 0x100000 with stride 0x20000.
- The arm,armv8-timer DT node declares clock-frequency 19200000 Hz.

### Configuration

- The board DTS includes pinned sm8250.dtsi, enables cpu0 and explicitly disables cpu1-cpu7. payload.kernel\_args includes cpus=1.
- ArmCpu and ArmDTPlatform are selected with their inherited archive recipes; timebase 19200000 and GIC resources come from the reviewed SoC DTSI.

### Procedure

- Collect /proc/cpuinfo, online/present CPU lists, cpufreq policies, /proc/interrupts and the live DT using the inventory script.

- The user observed automatic reset with the exact idle-return image 55b7c0354446888e80d0ed77258e4f29c236fdd9df6e65bfafbfab2cb72ab5ed. This confirms idle\_thread\_create returned; the next checkpoint is sched\_startup return, not initialize\_screen internals.
- The next image targets idle\_thread\_create return at 0xfffffff00798c8c4. Its modified kernel was independently extracted from the FAT payload and matched to the patch site. No result for this image has yet been observed.
- The user confirms the handed-off reset-before initialize\_screen probe did not reset. That probe resets immediately at the call site, so the next test isolates whether idle\_thread\_create returns; no conclusion about initialize\_screen internals is drawn.
- The first-boot DRAM window now excludes the entire observed camera CMA allocation window; CPU0-only topology and the disabled peripheral policy are unchanged.
- The board now includes disabled measured peripheral declarations alongside the unchanged CPU0-only topology; the checker compares their GIC interrupt cell translation without enabling peripheral drivers.
- Core identity differs in Affinity1 \(0x100 steps\); retaining only the lowest MPIDR byte would lose the recorded core distinction.
- Android's three frequency policies are not automatically the cluster layout required by the selected XNU profile.
- GICv3 arch\_timer and IPI counters are visible in Android; they do not demonstrate XNU secondary-core startup.
- Single-core board wiring is implemented before SMP. Shared secondary-core and cluster patches remain in their declared SoC series; no E/P tags or SMP execution are claimed.
- Host check compared every shared CPU affinity with the captured Android tree and decoded the ADT to confirm exactly one enabled CPU, affinity 0 and timebase 19200000 in little endian. This is not an interrupt/startup test.

### Completion criteria

- Each enabled XNU CPU reaches its checkpoint and handles timers and IPIs with the measured affinity and timer rate.

### Current limits

- Human-observed reset checkpoints establish idle\_thread\_create and sched\_startup return only; timer IRQs, IPI routing and secondary CPU startup remain unverified.
- CPU0 diagnostic execution is observed; full platform matching and runtime interrupt handling are not verified.

### Next action

- Qualify the bounded exception path with a deliberate BRK immediately after MMU enable, before C startup, then reassess the repaired kernel checkpoint.

## Memory and DMA {#memory}

`implemented`

### Hardware and dependencies

- Stock /proc/meminfo reports MemTotal: 7806144 kB and CmaTotal: 458752 kB.
- Live /memory reg tuples \(base + size\): 0x80000000 + 0x3bb00000, 0x180000000 + 0x100000000, 0xc0000000 + 0xc0000000.
- The live DT includes firmware reservations, display splash at 0x9c000000 + 0x2300000 and ramoops at 0x9fa00000 + 0x100000.
- Applications SMMU: 0x15000000 + 0x100000; GPU SMMU: 0x03da0000 + 0x10000. Both declare qcom,qsmmu-v500.

### Configuration

- DTS describes the measured-bank subrange 0x200000000+0x80000000 \(2 GiB\), excluding the complete camera CMA allocation window, and all 30 static reservations. Other DRAM remains in stock evidence.
- The firmware overlay supplies the original low-memory configuration/stack/FD map. No XNU DMA device or dynamic Android CMA allocator is enabled.

### Procedure

- The existing Android shell transcript records cat /proc/meminfo.
- Capture root-readable /proc/iomem and the live DT, then compare physical memory and reserved-memory reg values.

### Development findings

- The original 3 GiB and 4 GiB banks are adjacent, with a separate lower bank. Even before exclusions the maximum contiguous range is 7 GiB. The current loader requires one EFI conventional memory allocation. The largest actually available run is unmeasured.
- The 768 MiB v60 BOOT was repacked as v62 with a structurally verified 2 GiB DarwinBoot request. v57 through v59 still encode 768 MiB and fail full ADT decoding. v62 preserves the base kernel, arguments, trust cache, Data seed, firmware and DTB. At target120 XNU reports 0x79e30 managed pages, about 1950 MiB. This verifies the larger managed-memory window on the tested image.
- Isolated BOOTf6fd6636 changes only darwinboot-memory-size512-&gt;768MiB; final FAT round-trip, unchanged kernel/arguments/trust/seed/DTS/reservations pass. Device target60 main1/cpu8 and pages0x2b187 confirm about689.5MiB managed RAM. Free0x13b alone is not OOM evidence. Default remains512MiB and no high-memory map is enabled.
- BOOT3da265b4 target60 owner photo confirms main1/n67/cpu8/sec7, SpringBoard27/runningboardd26, Data0/policy0/stage4 and all-core positive timer/SGI snapshots. Backboardd not observed; free0x183 excludes reclaimable memory, not sole OOM proof. Sustained runtime, controlled CPU workload and desktop remain unverified. See runtime-cache60/device-result.json.
- After corrected carriers were backed up/updated and fully readback-verified under explicit authorization, BOOT097c4f8c owner photo shows memorystatus no-victim \(available3060pages\), rather than prior cache EACCES. This is not proof of full mapping/launchd main. Source retains73293824bytes of carrier plus another73293824bytes of duplicated manifest tables. Borrow only exact authenticated driver-owned boot-lifetime allocations, protect them readonly, retain separate private readonly key pages and reauthenticate; fallback sources still copy. Teardown never frees borrowed carrier. Actual configure/free host ASan/UBSan+mprotect lifecycle/failure tests pass; Normal BOOT12766f5f stalls with persistent bars over3minutes. Isolated BOOTef97eb6a resets about10seconds after magenta per owner, confirming at least one shared-policy vr\_open return but not success status. BOOTc544bae1 before-init-exec checkpoint immediately resets per owner, confirming the exec call boundary only; exec and launchd success remain unverified. Session stopped at owner request. EFI owned window remains256MiB.
- BOOT cfbd84ba photo confirms stage4: ID/VALID/S2CR accepted, direct FAULT restore200ff succeeded without fallback, allRestored/mappingsUnchanged=1 and GFSR=0. Six context states captured; CB0..4 already configured with FSR400 of unknown age, CB73 SCTLR3ab3a706 is not a verified free bank. No context writes, DMA or UFS commands occurred.
- Combined field diagnostic BOOT cfbd84ba34fc03b50661b46c9e22fe59c43ba651bca6967da49f1df1ec373e75 prepared from source7ee2918/kernel53e9e68c. Thirteen host tests, input-stable kernel build, independent FAT/arguments/FD checks and DT/ADT check passed. BOOT-only upload exited0; actual VALID/S2CR/restoration/context observations remain pending.
- Prepared a combined single-slot field qualification after ee45890a: test VALID only under unchanged FAULT policy; test S2CR only with SMR invalid; restore with a bounded Qualcomm-aware fallback if necessary; compare all protected mappings; only then read six context states. No UFS commands, DMA route grants, queue/context/global/clock/SCM writes. Field results and restore flags still require exact-image device observation.
- BOOT ee45890a device photo confirms stage5, inactive SMR56 ID a5 accepted and restored to zero; S2CR remains200ff, restoreOK and mappingsUnchanged are1, GFSR before/after zero and display04 unchanged. This qualifies only inactive ID-field writes, not VALID/S2CR/context/DMA, and contradicts blanket read-only claims for this measured field.
- Single-slot SMR ID test BOOT ee45890ab4875987b0e3376b095a64a5d58f1b23177c96b9ea4a996cd8ba9031 prepared from source 41d99e6 and kernel d455be28. Twelve host tests pass; compiled code has exactly two writes to offset958 with DSB after each. Packed FAT/arguments/FD, capacity and DT/ADT checks pass. BOOT-only patched Heimdall upload exited 0; device field acceptance/restoration observation is pending.
- Prepared a single-slot inactive SMR ID-field test, based on the complete 933a5d77 handoff observation. Requires exact measured geometry, clean GFSR, controller prerequisites, sole display mapping unchanged and slot 56 equal to zero/200ff. Rechecks mappings, writes only invalid ID a5 at offset958, restores zero and compares protected state; both writes fenced. No VALID/S2CR/CB/GCC/HCI/SCM/DMA changes. Outcome is not yet observed.
- BOOT 933a5d77 device screen is confirmed through END slots=87. Corrected SM8250 GCC gate passes; HCE=1 HCS=10f IS=400, both queue run-stop bits set and both doorbells zero, UTRLBA=9b296000. The sole valid SMR 84020820/S2CR 4 exactly matches the captured display SID 0820 mask 0402; preserve slot 04 and CB4. Candidate residues in invalid slots 02/03 are not active routes. No DMA, SCSI or root read was attempted.
- Prepared t970-ufs-handoff=1: corrected board-local SM8250 clock diagnostics from Samsung gcc-kona.c, removed SM8550-only memory-PHY GDSC reads, capture all bounded raw SMR/S2CR slots and guarded read-only HCI/queue state. No clock, PHY, SCM, SMMU, DMA or queue writes; use the exact tested baseline FD. Host and device results remain separate.
- User confirmed and photographed the expected T970 SMMUv2 RO stop for BOOT 7f729c2a: sCR0=002d0406 ID0=4c017e57 ID1=6000004a ID2=00005111 GFSR=0; 87 groups scanned, 74 context banks, no candidate match for 0060/00e0/04e0. MMIO observation is confirmed; SMR writability, actual UFS routing, DMA and root reads are not.
- Resumed at 47b113f. New default diagnostic t970-smmu-report=1 reads the measured SMMUv2 window before any controller/queue/DMA/root setup and stops on a labeled panic. It retires the unflashed all-slot write scan, separates sCR0 from ID0/1/2, honors EXIDS, and does not treat ignored writes or prior SCM markers as proof that secure-world routes are exhausted.
- Current platform checks passed in explicit firmware-envelope mode with the 256 MiB ADT owned-window request. Reserved holes are reported, not reclaimed; per-boot allocation-map capture is still pending.
- The handed-off diagnostic DTS uses the 0x80000000+0x80000000 ADT envelope to include the reported DarwinBoot window at 0xc0000000. It is explicitly tagged as firmware-envelope in the board checker, with a 256 MiB owned-window request matching the pinned loader default. Reserved holes are retained and not declared free.
- The pinned DarwinBoot AllocateWindow uses only EfiConventionalMemory and AllocatePages; boot\_args.MemorySize and MemorySizeActual describe that allocation, not all 2 GiB of the envelope. The checker retains strict bank/non-overlap checks for physical-bank mode and reports the envelope overlaps separately; actual per-boot EFI allocation remains a runtime measurement.
- The committed-source platform check passed with a 2 GiB measured-bank subrange, 30 static reservations, all 13 stock dynamic-pool windows, 45 stock XBL regions and seven disabled peripheral ranges.
- Committed-source checking found camera\_mem\_region allows allocations in 0x180000000+0x80000000. The first-boot DRAM window is therefore narrowed to the remaining measured subrange 0x200000000+0x80000000 \(2 GiB\), outside all 13 original dynamic-pool allocation windows. The checker verifies containment in a real bank rather than demanding the entire bank.
- The completed repository supplies the candidate bank and static reservations, and checks every original dynamic-pool allocation window and all 45 XBL platform regions against that bank. The firmware overlay owns its low-memory heap/FD/stack; real UEFI allocation still needs runtime evidence.
- The stock XBL describes 45 platform regions, including low-memory firmware heaps/FD/stack. Its DDR descriptors do not intersect the candidate high handoff bank. This is a static source comparison, not a GetMemoryMap result.
- MemTotal describes memory exposed to the running Android kernel; it does not establish installed DRAM capacity, physical banks or firmware reservations.
- CmaTotal describes the stock kernel's CMA pool; it is not a complete reserved-memory map for the XNU handoff.
- Several reserved-memory node unit addresses differ from their reg values, and some display ranges overlap; consume actual reg properties and reconcile their ownership before reuse.
- The shared ADT generator accepts exactly one contiguous memory range, so the three-bank Android memory node cannot be passed through unchanged.
- The candidate high bank does not intersect the captured static reservations. Dynamic size-only reservations and future firmware allocations are not thereby proven safe.
- Three dtc naming warnings are retained from the copied static reservations; the actual reg values and original names are preserved.
- Host check compared all 30 static reservation reg values, confirmed their non-overlap with the selected measured bank, and decoded the ADT DRAM base/size. Runtime allocations and DMA remain unverified.
- Step 3 audit records the candidate contiguous bank and 30 static reservations as partial DTS input completion. Dynamic reservations, UEFI allocation and full peripheral ownership are still not established.
- The UFS survey DMA buffer is allocated from the DarwinBoot owned window \(args-&gt;physBase/memSize\); the preflight refuses any allocation outside it, so the verified reads also confirm the owned-memory window arithmetic on hardware.

### Completion criteria

- The board memory description accounts for physical DRAM banks, firmware reservations and the UEFI-to-XNU handoff regions.

### Current limits

- The saved DT, XBL and iomem describe original software. New UEFI allocations, secure-world ownership, DMA widths and XNU cache/SMMU behavior require separate runtime checks.
- Removing board.example completes the concrete input configuration; it does not make the candidate RAM bank device-verified.

### Next action

- Capture the actual owned-window physical base/size and EFI memory map on the next observable boot; do not reclaim the rest of the ADT envelope.

## Storage and root {#storage}

`implemented`

Components: `ShengUfs`, `VirtioMmioBlock`, `RamBlock`, `VerifiedRoot`.

### Hardware and dependencies

- UFS controller: 0x01d84000 + 0x3000; UFS ICE: 0x01d90000 + 0x8000. Controller interrupt cells are &lt;0 0x109 4&gt;.
- UFS PHY: qcom,ufs-phy-qmp-v4 at 0x01d87000 + 0xe00; two lanes per direction. Clock, reset and supply references are preserved in the live DT.
- SAMSUNG KLUEG8UHDB-C2D1 revision 0090; logical and physical block size 4096 bytes. sda/sdb/sdc/sdd map to SCSI LUNs 0/1/2/3.
- boot=sda22 \(71303168 bytes\), recovery=sda23 \(86888448 bytes\), dtbo=sda21 \(10485760 bytes\), super=sda28 \(9961472000 bytes\), userdata=sda36 \(243668070400 bytes\).

### Configuration

- The Android by-name mapping and GPT provide measured partition inputs; no UniXNU root volume or writable Data region has been allocated.

### Procedure

- Read block sysfs and by-name links; read each LUN's primary GPT header and entry array and validate their CRC32 values without writing any block device.

### Development findings

- The matched v68 display comparison runs disposable RAM Data from copies of the existing 512 MiB seed. Both retain the Data UUID and disable the persistent backend to avoid reinitializing the existing UFS store. A direct v67 comparison therefore mixes Data history and policy.
- The v62 owner report shows Data mounted with 15402 free 4 KiB blocks \(about 60.2 MiB\) and 1115551 verified UFS blocks. There is no ENOSPC measurement. Data contents change across boots.
- The verified 512 MiB APFS seed reports 534294528 free bytes when attached read-only on the host, before any device writes. This is not runtime free space. BOOTe0a2d858 adds an opt-in bounded worker that checks the actual RAM Data device and requests APFS block counts, while preserving display counters in the 125-second terminal report. Host helper, BSD compile, kernel/prelink and packing checks pass; owner photo after successful BOOT upload shows rc0/phase1/support1d0 and 78135 available blocks of 4096 bytes \(320040960 bytes\). At the target125 snapshot the mounted RAM Data APFS has free capacity. See evidence/storage/data-space-v31.
- BOOT705d4160 mode6 owner photo02:38:12 captures all three processes/end marker unclipped. Constrained cap/active/busy=0/0/0; MG done35-&gt;37 while BB25/SB53 unchanged across 500ms deadlines. Runtime callback continues; investigate EDGE E/P counts versus current SMP topology before any scheduling change. See evidence/display/ui-queue-progress-v20/device-result.json.
- First boot of7e60f7c9 refused UFS handoff NOP at stage0, before NOP submission; CB0=100ff/1/e0, GFSR0 and historical FSR400. Owner rebooted the same image and root reads recovered. Exact stage0 predicate remains unknown; no storage gate or mappings were relaxed. Both photos are retained in evidence/cpu/smp-bringup/ipi-check/device-result.json.

Display backend remains disabled; next phase is display handoff/provider and independent staged SMP qualification.

- BOOT 933a5d77 device screen is confirmed through END slots=87. Corrected SM8250 GCC gate passes; HCE=1 HCS=10f IS=400, both queue run-stop bits set and both doorbells zero, UTRLBA=9b296000. - Prepared and independently verified BOOT 933a5d776edb2500be5c268b93e64f2cfec53fee6e12cebbb39869a8d7050deb from source 58bdac8 and kernel 147f8d3b; DT/ADT checks passed. - Prepared t970-ufs-handoff=1: corrected board-local SM8250 clock diagnostics from Samsung gcc-kona.c, removed SM8550-only memory-PHY GDSC reads, capture all bounded raw SMR/S2CR slots and guarded read-only HCI/queue state. No clock, PHY, SCM, SMMU, DMA or queue writes; use the exact tested baseline FD. Host and device results remain separate.
- Compared current T733 \(SM7325\) and umi \(SM8250\) public sources and maintainer-reported hardware results. T970 still inherits SM8550 clock diagnostic offsets; Samsung gcc-kona.c and umi agree on different SM8250 offsets. Current BoardInit skips GCC writes, so this is not proof of a reset cause. Public T733 firmware integration exists; no foreign SID or firmware was applied.
- User confirmed and photographed the expected T970 SMMUv2 RO stop for BOOT 7f729c2a: sCR0=002d0406 ID0=4c017e57 ID1=6000004a ID2=00005111 GFSR=0; 87 groups scanned, 74 context banks, no candidate match for 0060/00e0/04e0. MMIO observation is confirmed; SMR writability, actual UFS routing, DMA and root reads are not.
- Prepared BOOT 7f729c2a6ee8ee11b6eb5d4ff7ec23171b261854151578e36737d866a62391e2 from source 23d54b9 and kernel cca9bbb8. Independent FAT/kernel/arguments and packed FD checks passed, as did nine host tests and CPU0 DT/ADT checks. Patched Heimdall uploaded BOOT only with exit 0; device screen/register observation is pending. This is not a UFS/DMA/root-volume success.
- Resumed at 47b113f. New default diagnostic t970-smmu-report=1 reads the measured SMMUv2 window before any controller/queue/DMA/root setup and stops on a labeled panic. It retires the unflashed all-slot write scan, separates sCR0 from ID0/1/2, honors EXIDS, and does not treat ignored writes or prior SCM markers as proof that secure-world routes are exhausted.
- Disabled board UFS/PHY reg and interrupt overrides now preserve the exact stock ranges, including ICE. No root/Data policy or XNU storage driver is enabled.
- Primary GPT headers and entry-array CRCs passed for all four inspected LUNs; their entries and byte offsets are saved in gpt.json.
- Linux block sysfs start/size values use 512-byte units, while this device's GPT LBAs use 4096-byte logical blocks; convert explicitly when comparing them.
- Step 2 catalog review found ShengUfs declares SM8550/sheng clock, SMMU and userdata-layout dependencies. It and RamBlock/VirtIO/VerifiedRoot remain unselected until a T970 storage/root policy and interfaces are established.
- Step 6 storage inventory resolves the UFS controller \(0x1d84000+0x3000 and ICE 0x1d90000+0x8000\), QMP PHY v4 \(0x1d87000+0xe000\), all nine clock names to kona IDs \(UFS\_PHY\_AXI 167, AGGRE\_UFS\_PHY\_AXI 6, UFS\_PHY\_AHB 166, UNIPRO 179, ICE 170, TX/RX symbol clocks, RPMH\_CXO 0\), reset core\_reset=GCC\_UFS\_PHY\_BCR\(33\) and every regulator phandle; the stock UFS node has no iommus property.
- ShengUfs is not selected: it is scoped to the SM8550 controller with a 0x1d80000/0x2000 PHY, SM8550 clock policy, a fixed apps-SMMU stream 0x60 and sheng userdata LBAs. The T970 controller base matches but the PHY window, ICE block, SMMU ownership and userdata geometry do not.
- No SM8250/kona UFS implementation exists in the reviewed repositories: the shared SoC DTSI only declares disabled ufs/ufsphy nodes, and the SM8250 pipa port carries only package/SmmuBypass.h. UFS therefore stays disabled; storage remains planned pending a real driver port.
- Reviewed the SM8250 ports on unixnu.com/zh/devices: umi \(Mi 10\) has Native UFS, both root volumes and writable Data with launchd running; alioth \(Redmi K40\) marks storage device-verified using a device fork FFT-infra/NativeUfs-alioth pinned through feeds.toml; kebab reaches an HFS root; pipa is still stuck at root mount.
- The shared technique is firmware state takeover, not cold init: NativeUfs reads GCC gating and link state without writing clocks/reset/PHY, plans the two SM8250 UFS streams 0x0e0 and 0x4e0 from SMR/S2CR readback, and publishes a per-device userdata window. T970 uses the same controller 0x1d84000 and PHY 0x1d87000, so the umi/alioth driver is the concrete base for this port instead of ShengUfs SM8550 constants.
- Started the SM8250 UFS port by pinning feeds.toml ShengUfs to FFT-infra/NativeUfs-alioth@ab54059 \(device-verified storage fork, streams 0x0e0/0x4e0\). The component compiled; the kernel link then required the original IOStorageFamily absolute bindings \(scripts/build-storage-bindings.py plus scripts/data/native-storage-original-bindings.S, both stored here\) and the VerifiedRoot vr\_\* archive.
- Enabling the driver is blocked on two inputs that do not exist yet: the signed VerifiedRoot policy archive \(build-verified-root-probe.py needs the OS-volume verification bundle, not just the IPSW\) and a sheng userdata manifest that findRootLayout accepts; the T970 GPT is an Android layout. The integration is therefore prepared but disabled; CONFIG\_XNU\_NATIVE\_UFS stays off and board components/archives are unchanged.
- Built the T970 userdata-root image from the 21H461 IPSW with the board GPT geometry \(LBA 2969600..62458874, 4096-byte blocks\): OS e5634d04 at 0x100000 and SystemOS b4908cde at 0x16a600000, image fd43ee70, 9382658048 bytes. Added \[userdata\] and payload.adt root\_match\_volume\_uuid 04769D26-2CA0-4825-B443-E2E5F88300F2.
- The VerifiedRoot path cannot fit this board: the OS+SystemOS policy bundle embeds a 47 MiB block-hash table in the kernel \(kernel.macho 125374464 bytes\) and the packed boot image measured 138211328 bytes against the 71303168-byte BOOT partition. CONFIG\_XNU\_NATIVE\_UFS and CONFIG\_XNU\_VERIFIED\_ROOT\_PROBE are therefore left off; the next storage step is a T970 device-side UFS publication path that does not embed the verified-root hash table.
- Provisioned the T970 userdata partition without Odin: scripts/encode-userdata-sparse.py encodes one contiguous segment of userdata-root.img as an Android sparse image whose DONT\_CARE chunks leave the rest of the partition untouched, so three segments of \~2.8-3.1 GB each fit Heimdall's 4 GiB single-file protocol limit. All three uploads reported USERDATA upload successful.
- Enabled ShengUfs on the alioth fork with the empty-policy VerifiedRoot archive \(19 KB, native policy count zero\) and single default stream sid 0x0060. The kernel links and boots \(kernel a981a22d, boot image c38d09f7\). The driver reaches the SMMU setup but the SMR write for bank 73 slot 86 reads back zero, so UFS publication still fails at NativeUfs.cpp:455; the firmware-configured stream id or SMMU ownership is not yet identified.
- A t970-smmu-dump boot-argument diagnostic in arm\_init \(patches/xnu/t970-smmu-dump.patch\) dumps the firmware-configured apps\_smmu table before IOKit starts: IDR0 0x002d0405 and 26 valid SMR entries, none with SID 0x0060. The alioth driver then plans bank 73 slot 86 and its SMR write reads back zero, which points at SMMU power/clock or secure ownership rather than a missing stream id.
- The firmware DSC still states 'no UFS driver yet', and the ShengUfs hostBringUp only maps the controller: it assumes the boot firmware already powered UFS, the PHY and apps\_smmu. On T970 the SMMU stream writes do not stick \(SMR reads back zero\) and the IDR reads vary between boots, so the driver cannot create the bypass context it needs. The vendored board copy in package/ShengUfs \(IDR at 0x0/0x4/0x8 and a 0x820 attempt\) is kept for the next step but is not selected in the archive list.
- NativeUfs/ShengUfs reuse is not enough for T970 on its own: slot 4 is a wildcard SID mapping \(mask 0x402\) that the driver refuses, and the firmware-configured UFS stream has not been identified among the 26 entries.
- The vendored ShengUfs copy with IDR at 0x0/0x4/0x8 and an auto-reuse scan of every exact stream still fails: the planner rejects the block with 'SMMU AArch64 stage 1 unsupported' because IDR0 bit 30 is clear on T970. The alioth/SM8550 SMMU register model therefore does not describe SM8250 apps\_smmu; a correct SM8250 SMMU implementation \(or firmware-side stream setup\) is required before UFS can publish the partition.
- Restoring the Linux-validated SMMU model \(IDR at 0x20/0x24/0x28, NUMSMRG/NUMCB geometry\) showed the vendored driver's original offsets were correct; the failure is that no firmware stream is reusable and an SMR write for a new bypass context reads back zero. This is consistent with the secure world owning the stream registers on T970.
- Kernel-side SMMU programming is therefore not a viable path on this board: the storage driver needs the firmware to leave a bypass stream for UFS, which is what the alioth/T733 firmware does and what the Mu-Silicium T970 build does not.
- Keeping the SMMU map but disabling SMMU programming \(t970-ufs-no-smmu=1\) moves the failure past the SMMU panics and into an unexplained self-reset before console output. That is consistent with the driver's unbounded UFS completion loops waiting on DMA that the firmware stream mapping does not cover; kernel-side SMMU bypass alone is not sufficient.
- Route B started: scripts/gen-ufs-phy-tables.py extracts the SM8250 QMP UFS PHY sequences from the Linux driver into package/firmware/.../PlatformUfsLib/Sm8250UfsPhyTables.h \(SM8150 base tables plus the SM8250 HS-G4 overlays, 24/6/34/7 and 1/21/2 entries\). Wiring PlatformUfsLib and the Samsung UfsDxe is the next step and no firmware behaviour has changed yet.
- Both non-secure routes to a usable UFS SMMU context are now exhausted: kernel writes to the SMR are ignored, UEFI firmware PHY bring-up succeeds but UFS DMA still resets the board, and QCOM\_SCM restore\_sec\_cfg is not provided by this platform's secure monitor.
- The UEFI image survey \(t970-ufs-image=1\) completed on hardware after the full userdata rewrite: 14 UFS reads, healthy=1, poisoned=0, and all seven manifest/OS/SystemOS samples matched \(matchMask=0x7f\). It reuses the firmware SMMU handoff context \(CB0 0x100ff, SCTLR 0xe0, existing SMR entries\) and stops with noPublication=1, so no block device or root is published.
- The working UFS reads reuse the firmware handoff state rather than a driver-created SMMU context: CB0 with cbar=0x100ff, cba2r=1, sctlr=0xe0 and the firmware SMR entries for SID 0x0e0/0x4e0 in slots 55/56, with the DMA buffer inside the owned window. This is the configuration the root publication must reuse.
- Connected that handoff to the production publication path \(t970-ufs-publish=1\): hostBringUpHandoff maps the controller and SMMU, takes DMA inside the DarwinBoot owned window, requires the exact reviewed firmware state \(CB0 0x100ff/0x1/0xe0, 0x810 0x84020820, 0xc10 4, 0x954 0x800000e0, 0x958 0x800004e0, zero tails, GFSR/FSR zero\) and then runs the survey's NOP/power/TUR sequence without writing the SMMU, the queue registers or storage. startRoot keeps every window fail-closed, so a rejected window publishes nothing.
- The kernel still links the empty policy table, and the policy bundle cannot be moved into it: the OS window needs about 46.4 MB of block hashes and SystemOS about 25.2 MB \(32 bytes per 4096-byte block\), so the packed boot image would exceed the 71303168-byte BOOT partition. The carrier therefore has to move out of the kernel; the planned shape is both manifests in a fixed userdata region after the image, read at boot through the same handoff transport and verified by the compiled-in owner key before the policy table is filled.
- The first publication image \(BOOT 3b0d7d73\) was refused on hardware by its own precondition: CB0 was still active \(0x100ff/0x1/0xe0\), GFSR 0, but the two parked SID entries 0x954/0x958 read zero and CB0 FSR read 0x400. The survey's healthy\(\) snapshot was a state the survey itself had written \(its NOP test parks SID 0x0e0/0x4e0 there\) and FSR400 predates the kernel, so the snapshot was the wrong premise. The gate now checks fault drift around its own commands instead of a boot-specific snapshot, and the state words are reported without deciding.
- Its successor \(BOOT 11a1d635\) hung at the magenta loader bar with no panic text, which under t970-novc means a stall: the handoff path proved the transport without taking over the request list, so UTRLBA still addressed the firmware buffer and the first doorbell made the controller fetch foreign descriptors. The survey never hit this because its own NOP test halts the queue, writes UTRLBA/UTRLBAU to the owned DMA and restarts it. hostBringUpHandoff now performs that takeover and still writes no SMMU register.
- BOOT ca021c28 \(handoff bring-up reusing the survey's t970\_nop::run admission and NOP\) reached the owner policy on hardware: the production reader validated the SHNGROOT manifest \(status 0\), planned both windows with the exact host geometry \(OS 0x100000/6077580800, SystemOS 0x16a600000/3303014400\) and published nothing because the linked policy table is empty \(prepared=0 published=0, stage policy count invalid\). The survey sequence is what makes this board work; the two hand-written bring-up variants before it either refused a state the survey itself had created or stalled the machine.
- The policy carrier therefore has exact inputs: 1483785 OS blocks and 806400 SystemOS blocks, 73285920 bytes of block hashes plus two manifests, against a 71303168-byte BOOT partition. The root image ends at LBA 5260288, so a fixed region from LBA 5505024 \(about 18000 blocks\) after the image is the candidate carrier, read at boot through the same handoff transport and authenticated by the compiled-in owner key before the policy table is filled.
- Implemented the carrier on both sides. Host: scripts/build-owner-root-manifests.py streams each window out of the sparse segments \(no 9.4 GB file\), signs it with the owner key through kits/VerifiedRoot/tools/manifest.py, and authenticates the result with the real C code \(vr\_host\_open plus six sampled blocks\). OS is 1483785 blocks / 47481664 bytes at LBA 5505024 and SystemOS 806400 blocks / 25805344 bytes at LBA 5516624, total 73287008 bytes; the OS UUID is the ADT root match 04769D26-2CA0-4825-B443-E2E5F88300F2 and the SystemOS UUID 8846FE78-2910-4297-AF97-D59BB6F8FC16 is the mounted cryptex volume UUID, which equals the kernel's XNU\_OWNER\_CACHE\_VOLUME\_UUID. Kernel: the linked table and count moved to .data and the real 395-byte owner key anchor is linked instead of zeros; installOwnerPolicyCarrier reads each envelope through hostRead4k, authenticates it with vr\_open, and only then fills verified\_root\_policies, so a bad envelope still publishes nothing.
- Wrote both envelopes into the carrier region through the qualified recovery ADB channel \(BOOT e67fe744\): dd conv=fsync at LBA 5505024 and 5516624, block cache dropped, and the exact envelope bytes read back with matching SHA-256 \(45a69e42 and 869da36d\). Nothing outside the two ranges was written. The first readback compared whole 4096-byte blocks and failed only on the partial last block, so verification now hashes the exact envelope length.

### Completion criteria

- UFS reads return correct capacity, GPT and known blocks under XNU; the chosen root mounts and writes persist only inside the allocated Data region.

### Current limits

- Root image and corrected carrier writes were explicitly authorized, completed and readback-verified in their respective receipts. Readonly root and SystemOS preparation reached before-init-exec; writable Data, persistence and sustained XNU storage operation remain unverified.
- Current corrected runtime carriers authenticate the published OS/SystemOS windows; prior empty-policy publication reports are historical. Latest repeat has bootloader-only output, without a usable exec-return observation.

### Next action

- Track persistent Data separately from the v31 RAM Data result. Reopen the capacity hypothesis if a write operation reports ENOSPC or a later sample shows exhaustion.

## Display {#display}

`planned`

### Hardware and dependencies

- DRM controller is ae00000.qcom,mdss\_mdp; DSI-1 advertises 2800x1752 modes at 120 and 60 Hz. Android wm reports physical size 1752x2800 and density 340.
- Panel sysfs reports SDC\_801417. The selected boot argument is msm\_drm.dsi\_display0=ss\_dsi\_panel\_S6TUUM3\_AMSA24VU01\_WQXGA:.
- The selected S6TUUM3 DT panel declares dsi\_cmd\_mode, DCS brightness control, max level 355, four data lanes and 24 bits per pixel.

### Configuration

- The unbooted UEFI overlay uses the measured 2800x1752 panel geometry and original Display Reserved base; SimpleFb stride/pixel format and actual GOP service require hardware validation. No XNU display component is selected.

### Procedure

- Collect DRM modes, panel type and brightness limits; read only display-selection tokens from /proc/cmdline and match the selected S6TUUM3 node in the live DT.

### Development findings

- v68 compares matched RAM-Data BOOT images with and without the three SpringBoard idle/dim preferences used by Sirius. The 21H461 cache has direct references from SBIdleTimerDefaults and SBPowerDefaults. The BOOTs share kernel, ADT, trust cache, arguments and firmware. The owner reports that the treatment did not go black. At target360 there are 253 successful CPU copies and retirements with blank/dark=0/0; the last copy was at 379990 ms. The paired RAM control is still unbooted, so the effect cannot yet be attributed solely to the preferences. v69 removes only the terminal report. The owner confirms Hello remains visible; swipe and power press do not respond.
- v67 adds an opt-in root-domain watchdog panic detail with PM machine state, wait reason, current and requested power states, pending count and first pending service class. It also retains a 240-second frame report if no watchdog fires. Kernel prelink and final BOOT FAT checks pass. The device reaches target240 with a completed blank GOP fill 28 ms after the last normal CPU copy. No PM watchdog panic occurred before the report.
- v65 with noidle=1 still shows Hello and then turns black after about ten seconds. The owner photo shows no panic text. v66 uses the sleep-disabled ADT default also used on T733 and dada; it retains noidle and adds only that property to the tested payload.
- v64 owner photo confirms Sleep/Wake hang detected at IOPMrootDomain.cpp:12812. The linked stack reaches IOService::watchdog\_timer\_expired; this timer belongs to the PM root domain. The original triggering request and blocked child are unknown. v65 changes only noidle=1 on the continuous v63 image and preserves the power watchdog.
- v64 changes the board observation callbacks only in the reviewed display archive, then relinks and repacks the v63 layout with a 240-second runtime report. The new fields track the latest completed CPU copy, explicit blank fill and sampled black source. The source scan is bounded and occurs before copying.
- The owner target120 reference shows GOP published, three swaps/copies/retirements, first copy at 114.231 seconds and software vsync start1/arm0. SpringBoard and BackBoard are active, with no desktop observation. The photo has no matching upload receipt. v62 reaches 220 processes with four copies and SpringBoard S1 in a CFRunLoop Mach receive. Publish to first copy remains about 94 seconds. v63 removes the terminal report and reaches visible Hello setup per the owner. Animation stutters and the screen goes black about four seconds later. A short power press did not restore it. v64 adds last copy, blank-fill and dark-source timing in a 240-second diagnostic. The home screen remains unverified.
- V37 mode9 limits the report to two processes and one line per sampled thread. It records the current CPU scanout writer thread ID. Host row/line budget and final FAT checks pass. BOOTea3e7c10 owner photo shows both processes and clipped0. Hold0/writer0, two frame retirements. BackBoard samples setGrayscale and SwapEnd. SpringBoard samples the recurring LaunchServices lock and a synchronous Mach wait.
- V34 adds an opt-in terminal scanout hold synchronized with the Legacy work loop, and reports display publication and first CPU surface copy times. Host compile, prelink and final FAT checks passed. BOOTc9c49879 uploaded and reboot requested. Owner photo shows hold1 after bounded retries, publication at 18527 ms and first CPU copy at 143217 ms, with five retirements. BackBoard accessibility bundle code executes. Home screen remains unverified.
- V32 adds board-side counters for the existing GopFramebuffer software vsync events. BOOT6b692880 rebuilt and passed prelink and final FAT verification against v31, retaining the same arguments and non-kernel inputs. Owner photo shows timer start1/arm0/notify0, open7 and three successful frame retirements. At 60 Hz refresh0 matches the component gate.
- BOOTc96f86d7 is a 125-second comparison to BOOTbb4d8474 adding only a synthetic Wi-Fi address. Owner photo shows the same open7/swap2/copy2/retire2 as baseline; SB sample is in the Control Center default module-list property-list path versus a LaunchServices lookup in baseline. Independent live snapshots do not establish relative progress or causality. No confirmed UI improvement; stop repeating address comparisons. See evidence/display/wifi-address-125-v30.
- Original 21H461 MobileGestalt static control-flow audit confirms a conditional chosen Wi-Fi property path, sdio/wlan/marconi-wifi fallbacks and an IONetworkController-related fallback. An earlier branch can bypass it. This is not proof of runtime invocation, property acceptance, timeout, or the T970 seeding cause. See evidence/display/mobilegestalt-wifi-audit.
- BOOTfec656ec adds only a synthetic local Wi-Fi address property to observed BOOTaeb13ffc. At target90 owner photo still shows open5/swap0, lsd journal-recovery and XPC database-seeding waits, and SB BackBoard screen-info wait matching the baseline caller chains. Single independent samples occur before first frame, and runtime property consumption is unmeasured; no immediate improvement or definitive cause established. See evidence/display/wifi-address-control-v29.
- Mode8 BOOTf69965a7 target90 photo captures lsd threads5-7: thread5 handles an XPC request waiting for database seeding, threads6/7 are in workqueue entry; report end marker unclipped. The seeding worker and Wi-Fi lookup were not observed, and thread order can change across boots. See evidence/display/lsd-later-mode8-v28.
- Poco F1 iOS16 port reports the same Control Center/LaunchServices/unfair-lock caller chain and traced its stall through lsd seeding and a Wi-Fi address wait. Original 21H461 MobileGestalt contains relevant address and IONetworkController strings; actual T970 v24 ADT lacks both address properties. BOOTaeb13ffc target90 photo shows lsd7 threads, first4 sampled: install journal recovery/dispatch queue, inactive installs/dispatch group, and an XPC call waiting for database seeding. SB still waits for BackBoard screen info, swap0. lsd5-7, seeding owner and cause are unobserved; strings do not prove Wi-Fi lookup. See docs/ui-reference-comparison.md and evidence/display/springboard-mode7-v27.
- BOOTe82eec17 target300 owner photo shows main1, CPU8, open7, swap7/0, copy7 and retire7; upload was successful but not read back. SB waits on an unfair lock under the same LaunchServices/Control Center caller chain sampled in the independent target125 boot. BB first thread is in CFRunLoop Mach receive. These two snapshots neither prove continuous refresh nor establish a permanent deadlock. Owner requests shorter future waits. See evidence/display/display-late-v26.

After two failed pre-upload attempts the authorized BOOT upload succeeded without readback. Owner observed unchanged Apple logo for over three minutes following reboot, without other screen or reboot; background progress, frame updates and desktop remain unverified. check-platform stops before DT/ADT because the sibling framework HEAD differs from the reviewed pin. See evidence/display/display-continuous-v25.

- Human timing of about four seconds for console-state image 670e78ee17a752fc297dc6537a0c98d0074d8d191c599aa065804bd9ae6cb341 supports gc\_initialized low bit 0 and PE\_putc NULL before gc\_enable. This supports missing console initialization under the experimental skip patch, but does not identify the exact faulting instruction.
- Replaced the experimental skip-console patch with t970-console-bootstrap.patch: arm\_init now always installs PE\_init\_printf\(FALSE\) before PE\_create\_console, restoring callback and console/lock setup rather than continuing with uninitialized console state. Kernel rebuild and prelink passed; the final Mach-O has adjacent unconditional PE\_init\_printf\(FALSE\) and PE\_create\_console calls. Device return checkpoint remains pending.
- The handed-off t970-skip-early-console.patch keeps PE\_create\_console/PE\_init\_printf opt-in via t970-console as an experimental workaround. It does not prove an Apple-console hang; the latest user result places the unobserved checkpoint before initialize\_screen.
- The live DT contains many alternative panels; the boot selector identifies S6TUUM3 rather than the S6TUUM2 candidate.
- DRM mode geometry is landscape while wm and touch coordinates are portrait; display/input orientation must be mapped explicitly.
- At capture time DPMS was Off and brightness was zero; this records Android runtime state, not a display failure.
- GopFramebuffer is deferred at Step 2 because the T970 UEFI GOP handoff and display power callbacks have not been established.
- The bring-up console patch populates vinfo and maps the UEFI framebuffer only so the panic screen can draw text; no display driver, panel init or XNU framebuffer integration is implemented and display remains planned.

### Completion criteria

- A known image has correct geometry and colors, followed by working userland presentation and brightness control.

### Current limits

- Framebuffer physical address, stride, pixel format and UEFI GOP handoff are not verified by the mode list.

### Next action

- Keep v69 as the visible Setup baseline. Implement and verify touch and Setup completion separately. The v68 matched RAM-Data control is retained for attribution.
- V31 confirmed spare RAM Data capacity at target125. V35 reports hold0 and reproduces the v34 BB QuartzCore mutex and SB LaunchServices context waits. V36 shows SwapEnd and dispatch group wait in BackBoard, but screen clips before SpringBoard. V37 fits both processes on screen. Capture the resolved SpringBoard unfair-lock owner, then map that ID to the peer stacks. Compare source extent with a straight-on photo only if both show the same frame.

## Input {#input}

`planned`

### Hardware and dependencies

- Touch binds to fts\_touch \(stm,fts\_touch\), I2C bus 4 address 0x49 on controller 0x994000. DT IRQ GPIO is 15, with irq\_type 0x2008.
- Touch DT supplies are pm8150\_l14 and pm8150\_l13; firmware path is tsp\_stm/fts1ba90a\_gts7xl.bin. Android touch axes span X 0-1751 and Y 0-2799.
- S Pen binds to wacom\_w90xx \(wacom,w90xx\), I2C bus 9 address 0x56 on controller 0x88c000. DT IRQ GPIO is 136 and firmware path is epen/w9021\_gts7xl.bin.
- Wacom DT max\_coords=&lt;0x5880 0x3750&gt;, max\_pressure=4095 and invert=&lt;0 1 1&gt;; GPIO keys, power-key and hall inputs are also enumerated.

### Configuration

- The live DT records GENI I2C resources, supplies, IRQs and coordinate transforms; no XNU HID or touch component has been selected.

### Procedure

- Read /proc/bus/input/devices, getevent -lp and bound I2C driver links; correlate them with the exact touch and Wacom DT nodes.

### Development findings

- The stock fts1ba90a family uses command 0x22 followed by a five-byte identity read, expected bytes 39 36, and 16-byte FIFO records. The T970 firmware name and stock board wiring match this reference. v70 first samples GCC branch state and GPIO12/13/15 without transactions. It skips the GENI engine if any required clock reports off.
- v69 owner reports swipe and power-key presses do not affect the visible Hello screen. No XNU touch or key service has been wired for this image. Stock Android enumerates KEY\_POWER on PM8150 PON separately from touch.
- SM-T733 uses Focaltech over GENI SPI and separate PMIC-key and HID bridges. Its 21H461 touch HID descriptor and original IOHIDEventDriver dispatch bindings are ABI references; its Focaltech transactions, hardware geometry and key wiring need a separate board match.
- T970 touch I2C uses QUP SE5 at 0x994000 and GPIO12/13, with the stock GPI0 controller at 0x900000 and SMMU SID 0x5b6. The hardware-tested umi ST FTM5 path uses SE at 0xa94000 and a different GPI wrapper. Compare the exact T970 chip identity and event format before reusing the transport or HID path.
- The actual boot kernel contains all three named input firmware blobs. A unique common image base and 24-byte builtin\_fw layout are reconstructed through AArch64 RELATIVE relocations; source offsets, lengths and SHA256 are recorded in builtin-firmware.json.
- The disabled board I2C nodes describe measured controller/IRQ resources and input firmware names/coordinate properties; clock/regulator/HID implementations remain planned.
- The filesystem-only capture did not find the three input assets; the subsequent builtin-kernel inspection above resolves their source and hashes.
- The bound touch driver is STM FTS; the template's Novatek implementation is not established as compatible. Wacom reports a separate input device and requires its own protocol handling.
- Step 2 keeps ShengNovatekTouch, QcomGeniSpi and ShengHidInput unselected: their Novatek/SPI/sheng dependencies do not match the measured STM FTS/I2C and independent Wacom input paths.

### Completion criteria

- Touch contacts preserve stable IDs and releases, coordinates map correctly to the display, and keys/pen produce the expected HID reports.

### Current limits

- Firmware hashes and static I2C/IRQ inputs are established. Protocol transactions, sequencing and XNU event delivery remain untested; no input events were injected.

### Next action

- Temporarily vote SE5 and qualify the inherited GENI image with the known wrapper AHB votes. Restore the original clocks before proceeding to pins, rails, 0x49 identity and FIFO. Map PM8150 PON keys separately.

## GPU {#gpu}

`planned`

### Hardware and dependencies

- KGSL reports Adreno650v3, seven power levels and a maximum clock of 670000000 Hz; the GPU DT chipid is 0x06050002.
- GPU register window: 0x03d00000 + 0x40000. GMU register window: 0x03d6a000 + 0x30000; PDC windows are also present in the DT.
- a650\_gmu.bin is 41548 bytes with SHA256 df79fa8482fcc7587bb239b9c9e2b24bce4729893b990fe3f82b30da8175b01c.
- a650\_sqe.fw is 31632 bytes with SHA256 b4c27420fd6a5c6b511b254cc0fad8cdd40eea38ceb3418f895a1084f4460487.

### Configuration

- The live DT records GPU/GMU interrupts, clocks, supplies and SMMU resources; no UniXNU GPU backend or Metal ABI has been selected.

### Procedure

- Read KGSL identity and frequency nodes, list GPU firmware files and calculate the GMU/SQE hashes without executing GPU commands.

### Development findings

- Disabled GPU/GMU overrides now preserve the stock named register windows and interrupts. Unverified shared iommus references are removed; no GPU DMA mapping is invented.
- Six A650 files include GMU/SQE and the split signed ZAP image.
- The measured GPU is A650, so A740 register sequences and firmware cannot be assumed compatible. Presence of a615\_zap files alone does not establish which secure firmware the running driver loads.
- Known GMU/SQE files and hashes are listed in proprietary-files.txt as inventory only; no GPU component or consumption recipe is enabled.
- Step 2 retains the SoC A650Gmu operations as a future reference only. Adreno740 is scoped to A740/SM8550 and AdrenoMetal depends on that backend; neither is selected for T970.

### Completion criteria

- GMU boots, bounded GPU submissions and fences complete, memory mappings remain valid, and a known rendered image reaches a Metal client and display.

### Current limits

- No GMU bring-up, command submission, DMA/SMMU ownership or Metal rendering was tested under UniXNU.

### Next action

- Compare the measured A650 resources and firmware hashes with the candidate SM8250 GMU implementation and document the loaded secure-firmware path.

## Power {#power}

`planned`

### Hardware and dependencies

- Power-supply devices include max77705-charger, max77705-fuelgauge, pca9468-charger and sec-direct-charger.
- MAX77705 is at I2C bus 2 address 0x66 and PCA9468 at 0x57, behind controller 0x980000.
- The battery snapshot reports Li-ion, capacity=100, voltage\_now=4252000, temp=289 and charge\_full\_design=10090000; raw vendor values are preserved without assuming units.
- Android exposes power states freeze/mem and mem\_sleep choices s2idle/\[deep\]; CPU/GPU/DDR/WLAN thermal-zone names are recorded.

### Configuration

- Board regulator, RPMh and power-supply declarations are preserved in the live DT; no XNU power policy is configured.

### Procedure

- Read selected power-supply attributes, available sleep states and thermal-zone names; do not write charging, regulator or suspend controls.

### Development findings

- v67 adds an opt-in root-domain watchdog panic detail with PM machine state, wait reason, current and requested power states, pending count and first pending service class. It also retains a 240-second frame report if no watchdog fires. Kernel prelink and final BOOT FAT checks pass. The device reaches target240 with a completed blank GOP fill 28 ms after the last normal CPU copy. No PM watchdog panic occurred before the report.
- v65 with noidle=1 still shows Hello and then turns black after about ten seconds. The owner photo shows no panic text. v66 uses the sleep-disabled ADT default also used on T733 and dada; it retains noidle and adds only that property to the tested payload.
- v64 owner photo confirms Sleep/Wake hang detected at IOPMrootDomain.cpp:12812. The linked stack reaches IOService::watchdog\_timer\_expired; this timer belongs to the PM root domain. The original triggering request and blocked child are unknown. v65 changes only noidle=1 on the continuous v63 image and preserves the power watchdog.
- Advertised Android sleep states and power-supply entries do not establish UniXNU suspend, charging support or physical presence of every listed supply type.

### Completion criteria

- Battery units match measurements, charging and thermal controls operate correctly, and each supported sleep/resume transition restores dependent peripherals.

### Current limits

- Vendor battery units, firmware ownership and state transitions remain unverified; The v64 panic identifies a PM timeout but does not establish suspend or resume functionality.

### Next action

- Read the matching battery/charger driver sources to establish units and ownership, then map required regulators and clocks for the first boot path.

## Image assembly {#packaging}

`planned`

Components: `SoftwareAES`, `XnuPrelink`.

### Hardware and dependencies

- Local archive: ipsw/iPad\_Pro\_HFR\_17.7.11\_21H461\_Restore.ipsw.
- BuildManifest.plist reports ProductVersion 17.7.11 and ProductBuildVersion 21H461.
- BuildManifest.plist lists SupportedProductTypes iPad7,1, iPad7,2, iPad7,3 and iPad7,4.

### Configuration

- OS profile ipados-17.7.11-21H461 retains the reviewed XNU/Apple identity/prelink settings and archive recipes.
- package/firmware provides the concrete firmware overlay. Boot packing selects header v2, raw kernel, separate DTB at base+0x1f00000, kernel at base+0x8000 and ramdisk at base+0x2000000.

### Procedure

- Read BuildManifest.plist from the local IPSW using Python zipfile and plistlib to identify its OS version, build and product types.

### Development findings

- BOOTbb4d8474 owner observed Apple logo; photo14:40:13 shows swap2/0, transfer2, copies2, retire2. BOOT08b90255 previously panicked on uninitialized vc\_progress\_lock via first swap console handoff. Guard now matches t970-novc bootstrap. SpringBoard sample reaches LaunchServices database context from Control Center module discovery; sustained desktop pending. See evidence/display/display-novc-v24.
- BOOTe44011cb photo03:08:38 links BB wait to QuartzCore CAMetalContextCreate polling IOAcceleratorES, at most 300 sleeps of 100ms; SB waits for main screen info, open6/swap0. T733 48f documents CPU frames through NativeLegacyDisplay. BOOT08b90255 keeps kernel and changes report target to 125s; FAT comparison passes, ADT differs only in random-seed. See evidence/display/ui-cpu-fallback-v23.
- BOOT5385b6ce owner photo03:00:42 shows three pending queues drained, MG done134, BB161, SB75-&gt;78, display open6/fail0/swap0. No constrained head means Q cap zero is absent data, not a measured cached limit. v22 reuses exact v21 kernel with existing stack mode1 to locate current BB/SB main waits; host image checks pass, hardware stack result pending.
- v21 BCM2837 EDGE width fallback uses registered SMP CPU/cluster counts only when E/P counts are both zero. Previous BOOT705d4160 has all eight ADT CPU cluster-type absent \(SMP\), cached constrained cap0 and callback progress. Actual prepared-function ASan/UBSan tests cover SMP1/8, E/P precedence and non-BCM guard; kernel/prelink/FAT checks pass.
